"Ho toccato il fondo, adesso sto risalendo. Mi sento meglio, inseguo la felicità. Racconto la mia esperienza ai più giovani così che possa aiutarli a non sbagliare". Ho rischiato più volte di togliermi la vita, ero fuori controllo. La depressione e l’alcol sono due mostri che ti trascinano all’inferno”.

#2318. Silva and Karetsas ensure Dortmund defeat Real Betis as Guirassy limps off

Borussia Dortmund held out for a slender 2-1 victory over Champions League opponents Real Betis tonight. Despite losing Serhou Guirassy to an early injury, goals from Fábio Silva and Kostas Karetsas sufficiently outgunned their visitors from the Andalusian capital.

The hosts probably should have taken the lead with a pair of chances inside eight minutes. Twice, Serhou Guirassy failed to steer home right flank deliveries from Julian Ryerson and Jobe Bellingham. Barring an offside goal for Betis star Antony, BVB were largely on top.

Following a couple sighters from Felix Nmecha outside the box, Alvaro Vallés produced a point-blank save to keep Daniel Svensson out. Betis also appeared in the final third at the other end, notably Abdessamad Ezzalzouli testing Gregor Kobel with a curling strike.

Dortmund were dealt a blow after the half-hour when Guirassy left the field with a suspected injury. On came Kostas Karetsas in his place, but the half would end without any noteworthy moments as both Natan and Ramy Bensebaini picked up bookings for fouls.

Having knocked away on the door for most of the first-half, it took just 4 minutes for BVB to open the scoring after the break. Ryerson put a low cross into the box for Bellingham to strike. The rebound off Vallés fell nicely to Silva, thrashing in from six yards out.

It could have gotten better soon after as Karetsas failed to beat Vallés. For all the hosts' dominance, Betis grabbed a lifeline through substitute Aitor Ruibal. A costly Anton mistake surrendered the ball, enabling Ezzalzouli to find the winger at the back post who converted.

Parity existed for the guts of 5 minutes in the end. Having just pulled off another fine parry to keep our Bellingham, Vallés failed to stop Karetsas deceiving his reach with a low drive. Niko Kovač quickly looked for fresh legs in Maxi Beier and Carney Chukwuemeka.

The Westphalians had a nervous end to the night as Isco started to pull the strings off the bench. A handful of late corners fell to the club from Seville, including one that bounced right through the area. Ultimately, a vital win for BVB as their European fixtures swifty turn tricky.

#2317. James Milner runs New York Marathon in three hours and nine minutes - just months after retiring from football at 40 - after using AI training programme

By LUKE AUGUSTUS, ASSISTANT SPORTS EDITOR

Published: 07:49 EDT, 2 November 2026 | Updated: 07:49 EDT, 2 November 2026

James Milner may only be a few months retired as a professional footballer, but he showcased he is still an elite athlete after partaking in his first-ever marathon at the weekend.

Milner swapped his football boots for his running shoes on Sunday as he competed in the New York Marathon.

And the 40-year-old clocked an impressive time running the 26.2-mile route on his debut at three hours, nine minutes and four seconds.

The former Leeds, Newcastle, Aston Villa, Manchester City, Liverpool and Brighton midfielder was running for the James Milner Foundation - which has raised over £1.3million previously for various charities such as the NSPCC and Help for Heroes.

And speaking ahead of the race, Milner revealed that he had been using AI as his main training-base tool.

Asked about his long run in preparation for the New York Marathon, the former England international told Let's Run: 'Longest I've done is just over 32 kilometers. I hit 30 another couple of times, and then just used AI really for a training program.

James Milner ran the New York Marathon in three hours, nine minutes and four seconds

Milner only retired from football at the end of last season, finishing his career at Brighton

He won 11 major honours in his career, including the Champions League at Liverpool in 2018-19

'That's how it started, yeah. I know a few other people and stuff, but yeah, just bounced things off that really and went from there.'

It is unclear if all-time Premier League appearance holder Milner (658 games), who turns 41 in January, will run another marathon again in the future - but when that prospect was proposed to him, he revealed the training for it has helped him adjust to the realities of no longer playing professionally.

'I think it's one of them, after I've finished - hopefully - on Sunday, I'll either love it and have the bug and want to tick off loads of marathons, or maybe I'll hate it, never want to do another and maybe go to some triathlons or something else,' he continued.

'But fitness has always been a big part of my life, and I feel I've always been goal-orientated. I think I'd find it hard to not have a goal to train for, and I think this has been really important for me in terms of retiring and having something to aim for and training and still having that importance.

'Even when I was on holiday, [it was like] look, I need to get up and train for this. I need to eat the right things because I've got this run in the morning. I've lived that for 25 years. It's important for me to keep that going, and I feel very fortunate to be part of the New York Marathon. It's such a special city every time you're here.'

During a storied career, Milner won the Premier League twice with Manchester City before lifting the title as well as the Champions League with Liverpool.

In total Milner, who also had a loan spell at Swindon, won 11 major honours during his career alongside 61 England caps.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La partita si era messa male, abbiamo preso un gol da polli, ma la reazione è stata forte; la squadra si è aggrappata ad Isaksen, che in questo momento sta trascinando il gruppo. Il modo di giocare e la fiducia del tecnico stanno facendo la differenza. Ho sempre detto che non sapeva gestire i 90', faceva mezz'ora bene poi calava, ora invece arriva alla fine come se fosse il 70' e questo è un valore aggiunto.

Taylor è un giocatore dinamico, tecnico, completo. L'uscita di Fadera è stata un bene per noi, ci stava mettendo in difficoltà; è stato un vantaggio che abbiamo saputo sfruttare.  Serve la maturità per capire che non tutte le partite posso andare allo stesso modo, c'è stata pazienza ieri e non è facile perché a volte ti puoi innervosire. La Lazio mi ha dato l'impressione che sta maturando anche sotto questo suo aspetto, ha la consapevolezza che anche se va in svantaggio può poi vincere.  I cambi della Lazio? Si vedeva che alcuni calciatori, soprattutto quelli sostituiti, erano stanchi. L'allenatore deve fare delle scelte e mettere a disposizione i migliori del momento. Tavares era un po' affaticato rispetto alle gare precedenti, Frattesi giocava troppo vicino ai difensori centrali del Cagliari e così mancava qualcosa in mezzo al campo. Ci può stare, rientra tutto nella logica delle cose.

#2313. Baumgartner sets Leipzig's Champions League points target after City defeat

Christoph Baumgartner believes RB Leipzig will need around 10 points to keep their Champions League knockout hopes alive following Wednesday's defeat to Manchester City.

The Red Bulls fought back twice on the night, with Ridle Baku and Antonio Nusa both finding the net, before Erling Haaland found a late winner for the under scrutiny Citizens.

Speaking after the game, midfielder Baumgartner acknowledged that the European campaign has fallen short of expectations so far, with just three points on the board from four outings, but insisted there is still plenty to play for.

“I think we know that we're going to need around ten points. So we have to deliver, but I definitely believe we can do it,” the Austrian told DAZN after the match.

"I think the opponents we've faced haven't exactly been the easiest; we have to be honest about that.

“Of course, it's still going to be difficult, but we've seen that we're competitive. But at the end of the day, just being competitive isn't enough. You simply have to pick up points.”

Looking back at the evening, Baumgartner expressed his frustration, with Martin Demichelis' side fighting back from a deficit twice, but were unfortunately undone on each occasion.

“Extremely bitter,” said the midfielder. "I think we played really well for large parts of the game, gave everything, and it hurts so much to lose this match.

“In a game like this, at home, you absolutely have to take at least a point.”

Looking to bounce back, Leipzig are back in action against Augsburg before the international break, while they have to wait until the clash with RC Lens on 24 November, with the French side also desperate for a turnaround following their 9-1 defeat to Como.

#2311. Pioli: “Ci sono stati momenti in cui mi hanno chiamato tutti. Anche la Roma”

Stefano Pioli ex allenatore biancoceleste ha legato una delle pagine più esaltanti della sua carriera proprio alla panchina della Lazio, condotta nel 2014-2015 a uno straordinario terzo posto e a una finale di Coppa Italia persa contro la Juve. CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Ci sono stati momenti in cui mi hanno chiamato tutti. Anche la Roma, ma mi era appena arrivato il contratto dell’Al Nassr, ma non mi sono pentito di andare in Arabia. Volevo un’esperienza fuori. Hanno inciso l’aspetto economico e la possibilità di allenare Cristiano Ronaldo. Anche la panchina della Nazionale l’ho sfiorata e non da moltissimo.

#2310. Jack Grealish can't turn down a good night out and his attitude is a problem. IAN HERBERT reveals how the magnetic maverick star sparked concern and now faces a tragedy of missed opportunity

Published: 22:00 EDT, 29 October 2026 | Updated: 09:57 EDT, 2 November 2026

At the Pride of Britain awards a week back, we witnessed the brilliant and magnetic side of Jack Grealish, sprinkling stardust as he presented an award to a child of unimaginable courage who counts the Everton player as his hero. 'Footballers are classed as heroes – but these are the real heroes,' Grealish said that night. You could have heard a pin drop.

It was no different when he arrived back at Everton in September, to the delight of the kids awaiting him and his white Lamborghini in the country lane outside the club's Finch Farm training base. 'It feels so good. I think I'm at my best at everything in life when I feel loved,' Grealish said, articulating a very real sense that he was coming home.

He is popular around the club because it can be said without fear of exaggeration that he has a heart of gold.

Only on Thursday, an Everton-supporting father-of-three with a cancer diagnosis that gives him weeks to live disclosed that Grealish had put £10,000 his way. But within hours of that simple act of altruism coming to light, evidence of the other Grealish emerged, as Everton announced he had been excluded from the squad to face Newcastle in the Carabao Cup for failing to turn up for training last week.

It was by no means the first time. Everton sources say there had been similar causes for concern last season. Grealish reported sick ahead of last weekend's game at Arsenal, shortly after the Pride of Britain awards, and has breached Everton's disciplinary code through an unauthorised absence. The stated reason for his absence at Arsenal was illness.

Jack Grealish remains popular at Everton and has a heart of gold, shown by a £10,000 gift to a fan suffering from cancer, but he has since been dropped over a breach of discipline

There were similar causes for concern last season, with his return this summer going against David Moyes' better judgement

Everton's owners, looking for Grealish's glitz and glamour to gloss over their desperately poor and unambitious summer in the transfer window, were keen to bring him back. It was against David Moyes' own better judgement that he accepted his return as part of the deal that brought Iliman Ndiaye's unwelcome departure to Manchester City.

Moyes needed discipline in a thin squad where every player's contribution would count this season. It was an opportunity for the few star players to inspire and shine a light for the younger ones. And therein lies the tragedy of missed opportunity where Grealish is concerned.

He, the quintessential British maverick playmaker in the mould of George Best and Alan Hudson, could be a Goodison hero of these days, yet seems incapable of showing he is bothered, ridding himself of the acolytes and hangers on who are dragging him down, and appreciating what an opportunity football offers him.

Though he likes a drink, it's not so much booze as his attitude, and a struggle to turn down a good night out, which is contributing.

There is no doubt that he still has the skills to be a cherished player. That much was clear in the early stages of last season when he was providing more assists than any other Everton player until a broken foot requiring surgery brought a premature end to his season.

This season he has, as yet, no assists. He has perhaps been frustrated at the number of chances he has created not being converted, though not every winger has Erling Haaland as his support act.

Grealish's patchy attitude to a football life is affecting his game, as he is not taking his man on in the way he used to and does not emit such light on the wing

Look hard enough, though, and you'll see signs that his patchy attitude to a football life is affecting his game. He is not taking his man on in the way he used to and does not emit such light on the wing. Pep Guardiola, at Manchester City, was the first to see this and rapidly made it clear he wanted him gone.

It's not too late for the 31-year-old to become the totemic presence that so many fervently want him to be on Merseyside. Moyes, who sees the wasted potential, has given him abundant support and patience since City left him out in the cold, hoping that he can make him see the opportunity, here.

Thursday night's excellent Carabao Cup win over Newcastle United opens up the potential for an exciting Cup run, with Fleetwood Town awaiting in the quarter-finals. Everton have not won a trophy for 31 years.

The hope must be that the significantly public nature of Grealish's omission – very much a case of Moyes refusing to tolerate another breach – will shock him into action.

It was even the wish of Prime Minister Andy Burnham, present at Thursday night's match, that Grealish would be re-signed to Everton and the PM declared after meeting him at the Pride of Britain awards that the player was an example of the way people 'buy into Everton.'

The collective wish for him to thrive at the club could not be clearer. Only he can make that happen.

#2306. "I'm enjoying it" - Haaland reflects on remarkable scoring record against Leipzig

Erling Haaland has admitted that he enjoys facing RB Leipzig after once again proving decisive in Manchester City's Champions League victory on Wednesday.

The Norwegian striker scored the winning goal in the 78th minute on the night, as City came away from the Red Bull Arena with a 3-2 win.

For Haaland, it would be his 13th goal in nine appearances against Leipzig, having plagued the Red Bulls during spells with Borussia Dortmund and the Citizens.

Speaking to DAZN after the game, the 26-year-old was asked about his remarkable record.

“First of all, I've played plenty of times against them with Dortmund and City. I think every year we meet them,” Haaland said.

“And yeah, I mean, it's going well. I've been scoring a few goals against them. And yeah, it's always nice to play against them, let's be honest. I'm enjoying it.”

After extending his impressive record, the towering forward was full of praise for Leipzig, who twice fought back from behind before his late winner.

“I think they're a good team with good players. They always have good players, good talent. I think they've had this as long as I can remember,” he explained.

"It's never easy to come here. German teams are a bit different in a few things from English teams, and in a positive way.

“So we have to adapt a little bit, and we have to be at our best because in the Champions League, it's eight kind of finals in this group phase. So it's not easy.”

Next time out in the competition, Leipzig welcome RC Lens to Saxony, with the French side losing 9-1 to Como on Wednesday, while City prepare to welcome Italian giants Napoli.

#2305. Michael Carrick hits back at suspicions that he is on the brink of the sack at Man United - and insists he is relishing Tuesday's battle against Roma

Published: 10:06 EDT, 2 November 2026 | Updated: 10:07 EDT, 2 November 2026

Michael Carrick doesn’t feel like he is on his last legs as Manchester United head coach – but admits people might think he is ‘crazy’ for looking forward to the Champions League clash with Roma at Old Trafford on Tuesday night.

Speaking ahead of the game at Carrington on Monday, Carrick also insisted that he has no concerns over Lisandro Martinez’s commitment despite the Argentine’s reckless red card at Chelsea on Saturday.

United go into the game against Roma in danger of equalling a club record of ten consecutive games without a win in a single season set by Dave Sexton’s team in March 1978.

Last week, United bosses gave Carrick more time to turn the season, but there is a growing suspicion that he could be sacked in the international break after the next two games against Roma and Aston Villa.

Asked if he has the same feeling, Carrick said: ‘No, not at all. Listen. I can’t look into the future, and you never know what's going to happen. I don't know what's going to happen.

‘That doesn't change the way I feel. That doesn't change how we're preparing for the game tomorrow, and I'm actually really looking forward to the game. I can't wait for the game tomorrow.

Michael Carrick does not feel like he is on the brink of the sack at Manchester United

'You might think I'm crazy with the results we've had and what's going on, but I genuinely, really can't wait for the game tomorrow, and that's all I'm focused on, really I am.

‘Whatever happens, it is going to happen, but we want to make it a big night tomorrow night first.

‘It's what I am, it's what I live for, you know. A lot of people could do my job when you're winning a lot of football games. It's about finding the solutions, finding the answers, staying strong, leading right and getting the best out of the players.

‘We've got to keep pushing for that. But it doesn't change my mindset at all. I love being here, I love working with the players, and the struggles and the challenges, that just drives you on to want it even more.’

Martinez is available to face Roma but will be banned for the Villa game on Sunday after he was sent off for two bookings in the space of 82 seconds at Stamford Bridge – earning the second yellow card for striking Wesley Fofana in the face.

It followed the Argentine’s outburst against club bosses, and director of football Jason Wilcox in particular, after United were beaten by Atletico Madrid last month.

However, Carrick insisted there are no concerns over Martinez’s commitment or the general discipline in the dressing-room after Bruno Fernandes criticised his tactics in the defeat to Como a fortnight ago.

‘No, I've got no concerns about commitment at all,’ said Carrick. ‘Things happen in football. Sometimes you do things, sometimes you make a decision, sometimes you make mistakes, sometimes it goes well for you. But the commitment is not an issue for sure.

‘The dressing room is a good, good place, so perceptions are a funny thing. They can make things look all sorts of different ways and think about all sorts of different things. I've said it before, the commitment of the players to the club, to me, to each other, I don't question that at all. We need to play better, we can make better decisions, but commitment's not a thing at all.’

He says he is excited for Tuesday's Champions League clash against Roma - despite the potential for another embarrassment

Martinez and Fernandes are out of contract in June, although United have an option to extend both by 12 months. They are in talks with Fernandes but Martinez faces a more uncertain future at Old Trafford.

‘You know how discussions go and when anything's finalised, we'll let you know. But at this stage, there's nothing any further to update really,’ said Carrick.

Patrick Dorgu returned to training at Carrington on Monday after six weeks out of action but the Dane won’t be involved against Roma. Luke Shaw and Marcus Rashford missed the session and will remain on the sidelines with Benjamin Sesko and Manuel Ugarte.

United badly need a win after picking up three points from their first three games against Sabah, Atletico Madrid and Como. They sit 20th in the league phase. After Roma, they face Sporting Lisbon, RB Leipzig, Bayern Munich and Villarreal.

‘Of course we're looking for the win,’ said Carrick. ‘There is the bigger picture and it’s about getting through to the later stages. One or two games you can get away with really, as long as you make amends for that. Tomorrow and the games coming up are going to dictate that for us. So we're certainly still in it with a great chance of progressing, and it's got to be the focus for now.’

Roma’s only defeat in all competitions this season came in a dramatic 4-3 collapse to Real Madrid in the Champions League. But they have lost on all four of their visits to Old Trafford including a 7-1 defeat in 2007 – when Carrick scored twice – and 6-2 in 2021.

‘Long time ago, eh?’ smiled Carrick. ‘It was a magical night, I have to say. It was one of the best nights, one of the best games, one of the best atmospheres I've been fortunate enough to witness.

‘So the fact that I scored a couple and that was very unusual gives me great, great memories. But it was more the whole emotion of the night really. I have to say that it was a special one.’

"Questo entusiasmo ti deve far crescere ancora di più nella mentalità..."

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Primo posto in solitaria? Per me non cambia l’obiettivo, ma hai vinto bene una partita non facile. Hai preso punti sulle inseguitrici, ti sei allontanato ancora dal settimo posto. Isaksen mi è sempre piaciuto tanto, ho sempre detto che ha grandi qualità ma è timido, poco coraggioso. Sostengo da tempo che, trovando la chiave giusta, sarebbe diventato un grande giocatore e ora stiamo vedendo questo.

#2300. Heartwarming moment Luis Suarez spots former Liverpool team-mate's son - now Chelsea academy starlet - and poses for a photo

Published: 08:34 EDT, 2 November 2026 | Updated: 10:14 EDT, 2 November 2026

Luis Suarez shared a heartwarming moment with former Liverpool team-mate Glen Johnson's son Kobi while at Inter Miami's training complex in Fort Lauderdale.

The 39-year-old Uruguayan was at the ground for the Messi Cup, a youth tournament the MLS side is hosting, and spotted Kobi Johnson lining up with the rest of the Chelsea Under-14s, who are competing in the United States.

The former Liverpool and Barcelona striker went over to the youngster and said: 'I used to play with your dad, I've known you since you were little.'

The one-time Champions League winner then smiled for a photograph with the youngster.

When asked about the chance encounter, teenage Johnson said: 'He (Suarez) just said like, he remembers when I was a little boy and I was quite chubby, and then he asked for a photo.'

Johnson has been at Chelsea from the under-eights and has risen through the ranks, steadily gaining a reputation as an exciting young talent.

Suarez shared a sweet moment with Glen Johnson's son Kobi at Inter Miami's training ground in the US

Kobi Johnson is currently on the books at Chelsea where he is tipped to follow his father into professional football

Suarez and Johnson spent four seasons together at Liverpool between 2011 and 2015

And it is clear to see where he gets it from, with his father Glen having played 357 Premier League games at Chelsea, Liverpool, Portsmouth and Stoke, even winning the league with Chelsea in 2005 and 2006.

It was at Liverpool, however, where the right-back played with Suarez for four seasons between 2011 and 2015 - with the pair were part of a memorable Liverpool team that narrowly missed out on the title back in 2014 after the famous 'Gerrard slip' at Stamford Bridge.

It was during that period too that Suarez was accused of racially abusing former Manchester United left-back Patrice Evra during a Premier League grudge match between the two clubs in 2011.

Suarez was subsequently banned for eight matches after it was ruled that he had used racist language towards Evra.

Johnson controversially publicly defended him at the time, and years later, continued to be conflicted over the incident.

Speaking on talkSPORT in 2019, Johnson said: 'Carra (former team-mate Jamie Carragher) was right to apologise and I'd have to agree with him; I think the timing of it was wrong as the ban was so close.

'But in the club's defence, they were just trying to support one of their players.

'I don't think anyone in that dressing room or at the club believed Luis was racist or capable of being racist.'

While 42-year-old Johnson is enjoying his retirement after leaving the professional game for good in 2018, Suarez is still plying his trade in America at Inter Miami alongside other Barcelona legends like Sergio Busquets and Lionel Messi.

#2298. Demichelis insists Leipzig deserved more after Manchester City defeat

RB Leipzig head coach Martín Demichelis believes his side deserved more than a point following Wednesday's 3-2 Champions League defeat to Manchester City.

Despite producing an impressive display against the 2023 European champions, the Red Bulls were left empty-handed as Erling Haaland netted his 13th goal in nine outings against them.

Speaking to DAZN after the final whistle, former City defender Demichelis expressed his frustration at the result, but was full of praise for his players.

“That's football. I think, in my personal opinion, we deserved more than a point today,” the Argentine said.

"We produced an outstanding performance today against the team currently second in the Premier League, a team that will probably fight for titles until the very end.

“I believe today we produced our best performance without the ball, and we were also brave going forward with the ball, creating plenty of chances."

Looking back at how Leipzig lost, Demichelis was particularly frustrated by the way his side conceded.

“Look we conceded the first goal, which we scored ourselves,” he said, recalling Castello Lukeba's first-half own goal.

"At that moment, I don't think Manchester City had even a shot on goal.

“On the other hand, Haaland wasn't involved in the game, and in the end he scores the third goal. That's football.”

As a result of the defeat, Leipzig are left with just three points from their opening four outings on their return to European football. Still, Demichelis believes that the performance demonstrated that his side can compete at a very high level.

"I know that three points from four games isn't enough. But the way we played today, that's exactly what this team needs to show in every game.

"It was very bitter. It's a very sad moment for the players, but I simply congratulated the players. That's the way forward.

“And that's the kind of performance we need to show in this international competition. Today was a proper Champions League game.”

With the Red Bulls still harbouring hopes of reaching the playoffs, Demichelis urged his squad to “take confidence from a performance like that”, adding that Leipzig “always keep going.”

For now, attention turns to Augsburg on Saturday, before Leipzig have the chance to bounce back against RC Lens in Europe at the end of the month.

#2294. It's Golden Bells! England star Jude Bellingham follows David Beckham into underwear modelling in steamy Calvin Klein photoshoot

Published: 11:57 EDT, 2 November 2026 | Updated: 11:58 EDT, 2 November 2026

First there was Golden Balls... now there's Golden Bells!

England midfielder Jude Bellingham is starring in an eye-catching underwear campaign for Calvin Klein - almost two decades after David Beckham signed a famous £20m deal to become the face of Emporio Armani.

Real Madrid star Bellingham, one of the stars of England's World Cup campaign, shot the campaign for the fashion giants in the Spanish capital.

The 23-year-old posed in a series of pictures wearing the Icon Cotton Stretch underwear - which retail at around £44 for a pack of three.

'I really enjoyed working on this campaign,' said Bellingham. 'I liked the idea of "the icon underneath" – that confidence starts with feeling comfortable in yourself.'

His underwear campaign follows on from that of fellow Real Madrid and England star Beckham - famously labelled Golden Balls by his wife Victoria.

Beckham signed a three-year deal with Emporio Armani in 2007, shortly after moving to LA Galaxy, with the company revealing sales went up 20 per cent once the news was announced.

Jude Bellingham models the Calvin Klein briefs in a series of images released today

'Calvin Klein is such an iconic brand, so it was great to bring that idea to life in my own way.'

David Beckham models Armani underwear in an advert seen at Macy's in San Francisco

Bellingham celebrated his 50th goal for Real Madrid with a strike against Racing Santander

Bellingham scored his 50th goal for Real on Sunday, in his fourth season at the club since joining from Borussia Dortmund.

The star, who began his career at Birmingham City, netted in a 3-0 win at Racing Santander.

Wearing the No5 shirt in which Zinedine Zidane made his name, Bellingham has now scored one more goal than the great Frenchman for Madrid — in 76 fewer matches.

It comes after he scored seven goals at the World Cup - setting a record for the number scored in one tournament by an England player. It included the final goal in the third-place playoff against France, which England won 6-4.

'Reaching 50 goals with Real Madrid is very special,' said Bellingham. 'Not only for me and my dreams but also for the team.'

'It’s very important for all players to score goals and make a difference in games.'

#2293. Lazio, la forza dei cambi e un aspetto da correggere: dentro il primato

Otto gol, sei marcatori e due successi diversi: la squadra di Gattuso trova nuove risorse, ma concede ancora nel primo tempo.

La Lazio prima in classifica ha mostrato due modi diversi di vincere nella stessa settimana. A Sassuolo ha segnato sei volte, contro il Cagliari ha dovuto aspettare l’83’ per completare la rimonta. Due partite che, lette insieme, aiutano a capire cosa stia sostenendo il rendimento della squadra di Gennaro Gattuso.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il punto di partenza sono i 28 punti conquistati su 30 disponibili, con nove vittorie e un pareggio. Come certificato dalla Lega Serie A, si tratta della miglior partenza nella storia del club. Il confronto fra le ultime due gare aggiunge però qualcosa alla fotografia del primato: la capacità di trovare protagonisti e soluzioni differenti.

Il 6-2 contro il Sassuolo nasce da una partenza immediatamente produttiva. Taylor segna al 2’, Thorstvedt pareggia al 7’, poi Isaksen e Floriani Mussolini portano i biancocelesti sul 3-1 entro il 18’. La Lazio aveva dunque già costruito un doppio vantaggio prima dell’espulsione del centrocampista neroverde, arrivata al 31’.

La superiorità numerica resta un elemento da considerare nell’analisi del risultato. Non cancella quanto costruito prima, ma impedisce di leggere tutti i novanta minuti come un confronto nelle stesse condizioni. Dopo il momentaneo 2-3 di Esposito, Zaccagni ristabilisce le distanze prima dell’intervallo e Noslin completa il punteggio con una doppietta nella ripresa.

Contro il Cagliari, invece, la Lazio deve recuperare lo svantaggio firmato da Nzola. Isaksen pareggia al 36’, mentre la rete decisiva arriva soltanto nel finale con Pinamonti.

Il conteggio delle due gare restituisce otto gol e sei marcatori differenti: Taylor, Isaksen, Floriani Mussolini, Zaccagni, Noslin e Pinamonti. È un dato significativo sulla distribuzione delle reti. In questa settimana il risultato non è dipeso da un solo finalizzatore: hanno contribuito giocatori di reparti diversi, titolari e subentrati.

La vittoria sul Cagliari offre l’esempio più concreto del peso della panchina. Pinamonti e Pedraza, entrati entrambi al 55’, costruiscono l’azione del sorpasso: lo spagnolo serve il pallone che l’attaccante trasforma di testa all’83’. Con l’ingresso del centravanti, Noslin si sposta sulla fascia. Cambiano gli uomini e anche la disposizione offensiva. News

Come riportato da LazioPress.it nella conferenza del 1° novembre, Gattuso ha riconosciuto l’energia portata dai cambi, spiegando anche che la squadra aveva pagato qualcosa dopo aver schierato quasi sempre gli stessi nelle tre partite ravvicinate. La rimonta suggerisce quindi una risorsa importante: poter modificare una gara complicata attraverso le alternative disponibili.

Resta un aspetto sul quale lavorare. Tutti e tre i gol subiti nelle ultime due partite sono arrivati prima dell’intervallo, mentre entrambe le riprese si sono chiuse senza reti incassate. Due incontri non bastano a definire una tendenza, ma indicano una differenza da approfondire nella gestione delle fasi della gara.

La lettura del primato passa anche da qui: una Lazio capace di segnare con uomini diversi e di trovare risposte dalla panchina, che conserva margini di miglioramento. I risultati premiano il lavoro di Gattuso; il confronto fra Sassuolo e Cagliari mostra quanto contino anche le soluzioni a disposizione quando la partita prende una direzione inattesa.

#2292. UEFA Champions League: Tresoldi helps Brugge to third win, Kempf on target in Como’s record-breaking night, Real Madrid maintain perfect record

Nicolo Tresoldi's superb Champions League campaign added a new chapter as the German-Italian striker netted his fifth goal of the competition in Club Brugge's 2-1 victory over Napoli. The 22-year-old opened the scoring before Peter Bosz's side equalized with a goal from Kodai Sano, the younger brother of Mainz's midfielder Kaishu.

The Belgian champions took all three points as Carlos Forbs responded with his third goal in four games. Tresoldi isn't expected to be named in Jürgen Klopp's Germany squad for November's international break since he's yet to decide his international allegiance.

Tresoldi and Erling Haaland, who scored his 13th goal against RB Leipzig on Wednesday, are joint second in the scoring chart as Anastasios Douvikas doubled his tally with a hat-trick in Como’s historic 9-1 win at Lens on the night his compatriot Konstantinos Karetsas secured three points for Borussia Dortmund. The debutants became the first team to score nine times in a Champions League away fixture.

German defender Marc Oliver Kempf got his first European goal to help Cesc Fabregas’ side climb to fourth thanks to their superior goal advantage over the other eight teams currently on nine points, including 12th-placed Borussia Dortmund. The 31-year-old played for four clubs in his home country before joining the then-newly-promoted Como from 2. Bundesliga outfit Hertha BSC in 2024.

The defending champions PSG and Real Madrid are the only sides with a perfect record from four games. Former BVB midfielder Jude Bellingham was on the scoresheet for the record winners as they once again left it late to defeat AEK Athens in one of the early fixtures on Wednesday.

Kai Havertz was an unused substitute in Arsenal’s 2-1 win at Slavia Prague, while fellow Germany international Florian Wirtz played the last half hour in Liverpool's defeat at Fenerbahçe. FC Porto and debutants Viking FK started the night winless, but they climbed off the bottom three following 4-1 victories over Napoli and Sabah, respectively.

#2288. Puzzling Liverpool are not ready for the title, why Cole Palmer may have to leave Chelsea and Lisandro Martinez should have lost his spot long ago: IAN LADYMAN on My Football Weekend

Published: 07:09 EDT, 2 November 2026 | Updated: 12:20 EDT, 2 November 2026

In terms of points gathered, the Premier League title winner this season will be either Arsenal or Manchester City.

It's a two-horse race in which one of the horses has a bullet with its name on it sitting somewhere in a locked cabinet at Premier League headquarters.

We can presume, then, that Arsenal will win the title for the second season running.

Some are entertaining the idea that Brighton can have a go on our behalf. They have beaten Arsenal already at the Amex Stadium and now, after another brilliantly brave display at the Etihad on Saturday, they have beaten City too.

Fabian Hurzeler's team will not win the Premier League, though. Injuries, fluctuations in form and the fact they are playing in Europe will see to that.

Brighton have just played back-to-back games at City – the first in the Carabao Cup last week – and are off to Getafe in the Conference League on Thursday before facing Brentford back in Sussex on Sunday.

Liverpool are not ready to challenge for the Premier League title - and that is OK

It's exciting and it's fun but it's also a schedule that will get them in the end.

As for Liverpool, they are simply not ready. Not yet.

That was apparent before yesterday's home defeat to Arsenal, in which they played well.

Andoni Iraola's team also played nicely at home to City last month but lost that one too.

In fact, Liverpool have lost at home to both their rivals without scoring a goal. They don't score many against anybody, as it happens. Just 11 in the league which is three fewer than Ipswich and only one more than Manchester United.

So, no, Liverpool will not challenge for the title and that should be viewed as perfectly OK on Merseyside.

Liverpool have spent a lot of money since lifting the trophy under Arne Slot in 2025 but some of it has not been spent awfully well and last season was so very bad that they are coming from a long way back under their new manager.

There has been progress. Liverpool have recruited two sound defenders in Jeremy Jacquet and Ronaldo Araujo and are starting to see a return on Alexander Isak and, to a lesser degree, on Florian Wirtz, who has been nowhere near as bad this season as some people seem so keen to claim.

But Liverpool remain a puzzle in the final third. They play with nowhere near the structure and balance of Arsenal or the imagination and pace of City. Yesterday at Anfield was rather typical of how they can be. Possession, promise and power but a lack of calm intelligence and threat just when it really matters.

It may yet come. It is possible to see Iraola's influence. They would appear to have hired the right man to succeed Slot and that is half the battle.

But a third-placed finish, a journey to the last eight of the Champions League and a tilt at a domestic cup would represent progress for Liverpool.

As such – unless City escape their Premier League charges via appeal – this is Arsenal's title already and if that feels rather ho-hum and disappointing, it's for the rest of the competition to ask themselves how it has come to be this way.

Liverpool deserved to get something from the game at Anfield but warrant little sympathy for the manner in which they fell behind.

The home team's problems began when Virgil van Dijk took a goal kick and passed the ball a yard to his right to his goalkeeper Alisson Becker, who proceeded to give it away.

This is a common modern trend but no less confusing for it.

How have we reached a point where so many Premier League clubs seem happier for a goalkeeper to have the ball at his feet than they do an outfield player?

Van Dijk can pass the ball perfectly well. Let him do it!

Brighton are the league's top scorers with 24 and have had 11 different players on target so far.

That's more scorers than half the 20 Premier League clubs have scored goals.

Brighton celebrate a brilliant victory away at Man City - but they won't win the league

Nottingham Forest have to break free from their boom and bust cycle if they are ever to make serious Premier League progress and it feels as though that time is now.

Forest and their owner Evangelos Marinakis are on their fifth manager in the last 14 months. If normal rules apply, Oliver Glasner is in serious trouble after starting the season with just one win in nine games.

But Glasner was the manager Marinakis had wanted for quite some time. He first asked him to join in early 2024 at a time when Nuno Espirito Santo was struggling for results.

So now is the time for the Forest owner to look back at the churn of managers, players and indeed recruitment executives that has characterised his time at the City Ground and realise that something must change.

Glasner was unsparing in his criticism of the Forest players after Saturday's defeat at Brentford. In the same breath, he almost invited Marinakis to sack him.

But Forest's players could be forgiven for being confused and a little demotivated after so much change. Every time they lose back-to-back games, they must expect the manager to be sacked.

So it's time to shift that culture of uncertainty at Forest. Time to behave like a grown-up football club.

Glasner is extremely well paid by Marinakis. His annual salary is north of £10million a season and puts him up near the top of the list.

If Marinakis believes he has a manager of that standing, he should take his hands off the wheel and let him get on with it.

Oliver Glasner invited the sack from Nottingham Forest - but they should stick with him

Michael Carrick will do well to get much more time at Manchester United and there was a certain irony in Lisandro Martinez being the latest player to let him down as his team lost 2-0 at Chelsea.

Martinez – sent off after conceding a ludicrous penalty at Stamford Bridged – moaned publicly last month that United's recruitment hasn't been good enough in recent times.

Had it been any better, a seriously limited and unreliable central defender would have lost his place in the United first team a long time ago.

Carrick knows how this ends but what changes?

United actually played very well in the first half on Saturday and could easily have been two or three goals up by half-time, none of which will be of any comfort to their manager.

We have seen this movie before at Old Trafford.

Carrick will go, United's bunch of semi-serious footballers will raise their levels for a new manager and the club will start telling everyone they have cracked the code – again.

All of which goes to prove what we have known for a long time. Until the quality and indeed the mentality of the players changes at United then nothing else does.

Lisandro Martinez was the latest player to let Michael Carrick down away at Chelsea

Jamie Carragher believes Unai Emery is ready for a step up from Aston Villa to manage a club like United but has upset the Midlands club by saying so.

A Villa statement threatening to sue Carragher for his comments was simply embarrassing.

Carragher's language on the Stick to Football podcast was coarse and he may regret that but it was hard to take issue with his view that Villa sit below United in terms of profile and status.

Villa have made fabulous progress under Emery but part of becoming a really big club is learning how to behave like one.

Cole Palmer now has 50 Premier League goals in 105 games for Chelsea. Only Diego Costa and Jimmy Floyd Hasselbaink reached that milestone more quickly.

Time will tell, however, whether Chelsea really will provide him with the platform for success his elite level of talent really deserves.

For all that they seem to be moving in the right direction under Xabi Alonso, Saturday's performance against a team waiting to be beaten was still some way below the level a team needs if it is to launch a credible title challenge.

Can Chelsea provide the platform for Cole Palmer that his talent really deserves?

Palmer will be lucky to find his way into Thomas Tuchel's England squad that will be announced on Friday.

His form perhaps warrants it but Tuchel was unimpressed by his withdrawal from the last international gathering and is unlikely to have forgotten.

What, meanwhile, of Trent Alexander-Arnold?

With Reece James and Tino Livramento still injured, the return to fitness of Ben White and Djed Spence has nevertheless given Tuchel some options at right-back for big Nations League games at home to Croatia and away in Spain.

Jarell Quansah can expect to start in Madrid for the game that matters leaving Alexander-Arnold to yet again consider the wisdom of his switch from Liverpool to Real in the summer of 2025.

Alexander-Arnold actually started for Jose Mourinho as Real won 3-0 at Racing yesterday but it was only the fourth time he has done so this season.

At some stage, Alexander-Arnold is going to have to make a decision regarding his future.

Anyone who has seen Tottenham play this season could or should have told you that the improvement on last term was obvious, even when early results were poor.

The London club may well have paid top-heavy prices for new players such as Sandro Tonali and the Manchester City duo of Savio and Omar Marmoush and the value of those deals will become apparent over time.

But Roberto de Zerbi's squad has more depth and balance to it and is benefitting in particular from the improvement in players he inherited from Thomas Frank.

We all remember Antonin Kinsky's Champions League horror show as Spurs collapsed at Atletico Madrid last March but the 23-year-old goalkeeper has been excellent this term.

So, meanwhile, has former England international Conor Gallagher who looked like he had forgotten how to play on arrival last season but was impressive once again in Saturday's win over Crystal Palace, this time as a second-half substitute for Dominic Solanke.

The fuss made about some clubs failing to sell out every single home game is ridiculous given the financial strains currently being placed on families across the country.

Still, it's strange to note pockets of empty blue seats at Coventry matches given how long the club have waited for a top-flight return.

Home games are listed as sold out yet rarely seem to be. Fans say it's because it's too difficult to pass a season ticket on to a friend on days they can't make it.

It's not a good look for Coventry and feels like a problem that needs solving.

There have been regular pockets of empty blue seats at Coventry matches at home this season

Sunderland captain Granit Xhaka called on his players to show courage and play as they train after their defeat at Coventry pinned them to the foot of the table.

But the uncomfortable truth is that nobody in Regis le Bris's team has struggled for form more than the experienced midfielder in recent weeks.

Xhaka was a success story last season as Sunderland stunned the Premier League but needs to find some of that Player of the Year form quickly.

Published: 08:46 EDT, 2 November 2026 | Updated: 12:30 EDT, 2 November 2026

Aston Villa have dropped their threat of legal action against Jamie Carragher after the pundit apologised for telling the club's fans to 'shut the f*** up'.

The Premier League club hit out at the former Liverpool defender for making what they labelled as 'unacceptable insults' on the Overlap podcast last week.

The row had centred on comments made by Carragher in which he pushed the idea of Aston Villa boss Unai Emery becoming the next Manchester United manager.

It escalated further when Carragher doubled down, telling Villa fans to 'shut the f*** up' as he claimed that Emery would be interested in managing a 'giant' again and declared 'There is a chain in football.'

On Friday, Aston Villa shared a statement on club channels carrying the name of co-owner Nassef Sawiris, reading: 'Aston Villa will initiate legal action against Jamie Carragher for his unacceptable insults about the Club and its fans.

'Aston Villa will not allow anyone to stop our ascension in English and European football. Nassef Sawiris, Chairman.'

Aston Villa have dropped their threat of legal action against pundit Jamie Carragher

Carragher had been pushing Unai Emery towards the Manchester United job on The Overlap podcast and claimed that Villa 'will never be' United. (Source: TheOverlap/YouTube)

Aston Villa had threatened legal action on Friday, with Carragher apologising the following day

Carragher issued an apology on Saturday afternoon in an attempt to defuse the situation.

'I'd like to apologise to Aston Villa, the owners & their fans,' Carragher wrote on social media.

'My language this week was clumsy & wrong. I can assure Villa fans it was tongue in cheek but I can understand it's not come across that way, so apologies. Good luck today UTV!'

Fans were left stunned at the response by Villa to Carragher's comments, seemingly innocuous linking Emery, who is admired at Old Trafford, to the Manchester United job, which may soon be available with immense pressure on Michael Carrick.

‘I think Unai Emery is unbelievable with what he does year after year. That amount of change, players needing time to settle and he does it year after year,' Carragher had said.

'But the reason I’m going to go back at Villa fans is I can’t believe that a manager who is so successful in the Europa League, take a player, if you’d won the Europa League three or four times (Emery has won it five times) surely you’ll then want to go for the Champions League.

‘Aston Villa will never be Manchester United. I just think he didn’t do well at PSG or Arsenal, and maybe that’s because of egos in the dressing room and that sort of thing.

'But surely he would want that opportunity to manage a giant again. There is a chain in football, whether you like it or not.’

#2269. Ridle Baku pleased with Leipzig's performance against Man City and his goal

RB Leipzig's winning home streak ended on Wednesday as they lost once again to Manchester City in the Champions League. Martin Demichelis’ men came from behind twice before Erling Haaland's 78th minute strike handed three points for the visitors.

“We really wanted to win this home game today,” Leipzig's fullback Ridle Baku said in a post-match interview with DAZN. “We'd been on a good run at home with a lot of victories, and we wanted to build on that today.

“But against an opponent like that, it really comes down to fine margins. We weren't 100 % there in those moments, and we didn't defend properly. But overall, I think it was a good team performance that we can build on.”

Baku is enjoying his best goalscoring season in Leipzig colors, despite playing only 15 minutes from the last three Bundesliga matches. The 28-year-old scored his 30th goal in professional football with the help of Gigi Donnarumma howler in Manchester City’s goal.

“It's always good when you can perform and get the opportunity to play,” Baku spoke about his achievement and the recent situation. “I'm obviously very happy about the goal. But the team's performance is more important to me personally. I would rather have had the three points today.”

Leipzig's remaining four games will be against teams currently sitting outside the top 16 as they will need to improve their record of three points from four matches. The eight-time Germany international continued his praise of Leipzig's confident display against the 2022/23 Champions League winners.

“Overall I'm really, really pleased with the performance. We definitely still have several Champions League games that we can win. As I said, there were a lot of positive things that we showed on the pitch today. Now it's simply about continuing to produce those things consistently.”

RB Leipzig won three games in the row before losing for the first time at RedBull Arena on Wednesday. They will hope to continue their perfect home record against an out-of-form Augsburg side before the upcoming international break.

"On Saturday, at home against Augsburg, we can do better and hopefully pick up the next three points.”

#2263. Paolo Zanetti è il nuovo allenatore dell'Udinese

Dopo l'esonero di Kosta Runjaic, l'Udinese ha ufficializzato l'arrivo del nuovo allenatore

Dopo l'esonero di Kosta Runjaic, l'Udinese ha ufficializzato l'arrivo del nuovo allenatore, Paolo Zanetti.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Udinese Calcio è lieta di annunciare di aver affidato a Paolo Zanetti la guida tecnica della prima squadra. Ha firmato un contratto fino al 30 giugno 2027.Nato a Valdagno, in provincia di Vicenza, il 16 dicembre 1982, Zanetti vanta una carriera da calciatore, durante la quale ha vestito, tra le altre, le maglie di Torino e Atalanta, collezionando anche cinque presenze con la Nazionale Under 21. Appesi gli scarpini al chiodo, ha intrapreso immediatamente la carriera da allenatore, guidando la formazione Berretti della Reggiana a soli 33 anni. Il suo percorso nel calcio professionistico inizia nella stagione 2017/2018 alla guida del Südtirol, con cui ottiene risultati importanti che, dopo due stagioni a Bolzano, gli valgono la chiamata dell’Ascoli in Serie B. La stagione della consacrazione è la 2020/2021, quando conduce il Venezia alla promozione in Serie A, riportando i lagunari nella massima serie dopo 19 anni di assenza. Zanetti resta sulla panchina del Venezia anche nella stagione successiva, prima di trasferirsi all’Empoli, con cui conquista una tranquilla salvezza nell’annata 2022/2023. La sua ultima esperienza in Serie A è quella sulla panchina del Verona, squadra che conduce al quattordicesimo posto nella stagione 2024/2025 e con cui rimane fino al febbraio 2026.Ora per Paolo Zanetti si apre una nuova sfida: guidare l’Udinese e iniziare un nuovo capitolo della sua carriera in bianconero.Il nuovo allenatore dirigerà domani pomeriggio la prima seduta di allenamento. La conferenza stampa di presentazione si terrà mercoledì alle ore 15.00.A mister Zanetti e a tutto il suo gruppo di lavoro va il caloroso benvenuto da parte dell’intero club.Benvenuto a Udine, mister!

#2262. INTERVIEW: Club CEO Cramer feels Dortmund's "closeness" to its fans outshines silverware drought

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews) Despite having only laid claim to a pair of DFB-Pokal cups in the last decade, Borussia Dortmund's CEO Carsten Cramer feels the club offers “closeness” like none other as it's main selling point.

“It’s a challenge, but I think in times like this, people are not looking for a club like Dortmund but they honour the way we act as a club," Cramer told Bulinews.com and others at a media round regarding grown their fan base.

“When we go to the US or Japan [in pre-season] we always try and open the doors to the club. We want to make fans feel they are part of our club which is something that is slightly different to the others.

“They [other big clubs] are slightly more business driven or entertainment driven. We try make good stuff on the digital channels to invite them [international supporters] to be part of our club.

“If they are only interested in trophies or the player in black and yellow with the Ballon d’Or, maybe that’s something we can’t offer to them. But we are able to offer a different approach and closeness – that’s the Dortmund way.

“I think even hardcore supporters of one club do have a sympathy for us as a second club [to support], which we don’t complain about. We are not too small to be a niche club, but we are a for sure a bit different," he added.

When asked as to how the Westphalian outfit can improve their relationship with fans abroad, Cramer pointed to the supporters themselves. He feels their reviews often help BVB shape their approach around the globe.

“They open our eyes to the needs and demands which is very useful [to the club]. I also love to travel around the world because the feedback I receive about the club is always extremely positive.

“People love how we honour our fans and supporters. They always give positive feedback that the efforts we make are worth it for them. We also have to expand our international market to generate growth.

“In Germany everyone knows is. The [financial] cake is divided evenly, so we know there won’t be a bigger piece for us. That way the potential for growth is given to the international work we have to do," the BVB chief concluded.

#2261. Calvarese: "Fuorigioco Pio Esposito? Possiamo discuterne ma finché la regola è questa..."

Calvarese: "Fuorigioco Pio Esposito? Possiamo discuterne ma finché la regola è questa..."

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Sulle colonne de La Gazzetta dello Sport, Giampaolo Calvarese, ex arbitro, ha analizzato così il gol annullato ieri a Pio Esposito per un fuorigioco di millimetri: "Sul fuorigioco, per me, la tecnologia è stata una salvezza. Nel calcio di oggi, con interessi enormi in gioco, non possiamo accettare che un gol sia convalidato o annullato per un errore sulla posizione geografica di un calciatore.

Il fuorigioco semiautomatico (Saot) ci permette di lavorare con una precisione sempre maggiore. Possiamo discutere se ci piaccia vedere una rete annullata per una porzione di spalla, ma finché questa è la regola, la linea dev'essere collocata da qualche parte. Dura lex, sed lex.

C’è chi propone una tolleranza di cinque o dieci centimetri. Ma il sesto o l’undicesimo centimetro diventerebbero subito il nuovo motivo di polemica. Anche l’idea di Wenger, quella di introdurre la "luce" tra attaccante e difendente, potrebbe essere più facile da interpretare per i calciatori, ma non eliminerebbe il confine: sposterebbe soltanto il centimetro decisivo".

#2256. Cragnotti critica la protesta dei tifosi Lazio all’Olimpico

L’ex presidente, tornato allo stadio per la vittoria sul Cagliari, distingue la contestazione a Lotito dal sostegno alla squadra.

Sergio Cragnotti non condivide la protesta dei tifosi Lazio che lascia vuoti gli spalti dell’Olimpico. L’ex presidente biancoceleste è tornato allo stadio per assistere alla vittoria contro il Cagliari e ha preso posizione sulla contestazione a Claudio Lotito. Sulla scelta di non sostenere la squadra in casa il suo giudizio è netto: «Sono in totale disaccordo».

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Cragnotti, intervistato da La Gazzetta dello Sport, non mette in discussione il diritto dei sostenitori di manifestare il proprio dissenso. «Le contestazioni ci possono stare, io ne ho subite anche di dure», ha spiegato. A non convincerlo è la decisione di lasciare vuoti gli spalti mentre la Lazio continua a vincere.

Per l’ex presidente, la contrarietà alla società dovrebbe trovare spazio in altre sedi, senza togliere ai calciatori l’appoggio del pubblico all’Olimpico. A suo giudizio, l’assenza dei tifosi rischia di penalizzare proprio i giocatori protagonisti di un avvio che considera straordinario.

Nel motivare la propria posizione, Cragnotti è tornato alle proteste vissute durante la sua presidenza. Tra gli episodi ricordati c’è la contestazione legata alla possibile cessione di Giuseppe Signori al Parma.

Furono momenti di forte tensione, ma, secondo il ricordo dell’ex presidente, i sostenitori non abbandonarono mai l’Olimpico. È questa la differenza che Cragnotti individua rispetto all’attuale forma di protesta: contestare la società senza far mancare la presenza allo stadio.

#2252. Pinamonti segna e pesa: il 52% dei suoi gol è stato decisivo

Il gol al Cagliari aggiorna il bilancio: dal 2021/22, 26 delle sue 50 reti in Serie A hanno inciso sul risultato.

Non conta soltanto quanto segna un attaccante, ma anche quanto incidono le sue reti. Andrea Pinamonti offre una fotografia significativa sotto questo aspetto: dalla stagione 2021/22, il 52% dei suoi gol in Serie A è risultato decisivo.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il centravanti ha realizzato 26 reti decisive su 50 complessive, contribuendo a un saldo di 38 punti. Nel conteggio rientra anche il gol del definitivo 2-1 della Lazio contro il Cagliari.

Il periodo analizzato parte dal 2021/22, stagione nella quale l’attaccante classe 1999 ha raggiunto per la prima volta la doppia cifra di reti in Serie A.

Da allora, più della metà dei suoi gol ha avuto un peso sul risultato. Il dato dei 38 punti va letto secondo il criterio indicato nella rilevazione: rappresenta la differenza rispetto ai punti che le sue squadre avrebbero ottenuto sottraendo le reti considerate decisive.

Una statistica che mette in evidenza l’incisività del centravanti e aggiunge una chiave di lettura al suo rendimento realizzativo.

Il contributo più consistente arriva dall’esperienza al Sassuolo: 25 gol complessivi, dei quali 13 decisivi, per un totale di 20 punti attribuiti alle sue marcature.

Con l’Empoli, Pinamonti ha invece firmato 13 reti: 7 decisive, con un’incidenza di 10 punti. Al Genoa, il bilancio riportato è di 10 gol, dei quali 5 decisivi, per 6 punti.

Tre esperienze che, insieme ai primi numeri in biancoceleste, compongono il totale delle 50 reti prese in esame.

Il capitolo Lazio conta finora 2 gol, con una rete decisiva e 2 punti attribuiti alle marcature dell’attaccante.

A incidere è il sigillo del 2-1 contro il Cagliari: sottraendo quella rete, il risultato sarebbe stato un pareggio. Da qui la differenza tra il punto della parità e i tre della vittoria.

Per Pinamonti, dunque, una rete che alimenta il bottino personale e soprattutto sposta il risultato della squadra. È proprio questo il filo conduttore della statistica: 26 volte su 50, i suoi gol hanno fatto la differenza.

#2250. Mikel Arteta convinced his Arsenal players he could read their minds before north London derby win - before revealing the truth behind bizarre stunt

Published: 12:31 EDT, 2 November 2026 | Updated: 12:35 EDT, 2 November 2026

Mikel Arteta convinced his Arsenal squad that he could read their minds in what proved to be a successful strategy ahead of the north London derby four years ago.

Revealed in Daily Telegraph reporter Sam Dean's book Arsenal: The Inside Story of Arsenal's Rebirth Under Mikel Arteta, the Spanish head coach played the trick on his players in the team hotel ahead of the 3-1 win at the Emirates on October 1, 2022.

Arteta, who is notorious for his use of offbeat coaching methods, reportedly asked the players to hide an object in one of their shoes and told them that he would be able to guess whose it was simply by looking into their eyes and reading their body language.

He promised the team that if he failed to guess the player he would take them and their families out for dinner, but if he managed to figure it out, they would be paying for him.

After he and his coaches left the room to let the players hide the object, he returned and studied each of the players, and 'even checked their pulses, supposedly to see which one of them was panicking the most', Dean writes.

When he guessed correctly that it was Brazilian defender Gabriel, the team reportedly 'went mental', a source in the book said.

Arteta convinced his players that he could read their minds ahead of their 3-1 win against Tottenham at the Emirates on October 1, 2022

Arteta is notorious for his use of offbeat coaching methods in order to gain an edge over rivals

Later that day, after goals from Thomas Partey, Gabriel Jesus and Granit Xhaka secured the three points against their north London rivals, Arteta and his staff revealed that they had seen them pick the player through CCTV cameras at the hotel.

This wasn't the first time that Arteta has deployed an unusual tactic with the hope of gaining an edge over his rivals.

Ahead of a game against Liverpool at Anfield during the 2021-22 season, Arteta blasted 'You'll Never Walk Alone' over speakers during training to prepare his players for the boisterous atmosphere.

In the summer of 2024, he hired a pickpocket to steal from the squad during an evening meal.

The pickpocket was disguised as a waiter during the meal and, after the dinner, Arteta held a pre-match team meeting where, at the end of it, he presented his players' stolen items at the front of the room.

That ploy was in preparation for a match against a team who tried every possible tactic to gain an edge, and Arteta wanted to adopt a 'pickpocket mentality', to always be aware of people trying to steal advantages from them.

For another game, Arteta did a pre-match team talk involving a lightbulb, as he wanted his players to shine and emit energy and light to one another.

As Arsenal pushed to end their long wait for a Premier League title, the club also installed the outline of the trophy in the entrance of their main building at the training ground. It was finally lit up in May, when the feat was achieved.

And during pre-season just past, Arteta revealed that he deliberately made his team late for matches by changing their route or meal times, as well as making the changing room hotter and reducing the number of massage beds in the hope of preparing the squad for difficulties on the road.

After signing new terms last month, Arteta revealed that his quirky practices began on his very first day on the job back in December 2019.

The then 37-year-old claimed he 'destroyed' an education centre at Arsenal's training centre, throwing 'the computers, the laptops, the cables' on the floor. He then requested his staff to reorganise the office in no time at all in order to prove their togetherness.

As Arsenal pushed to end their long wait for a Premier League title, the club also installed the outline of the trophy in the entrance of their main building at the training ground

In an interview with The Athletic, Arteta explained: 'I said, "Listen, this is what I heard. I don’t know if it’s true. But from a lot of different people, the sentiment is that this is how they feel about working in the organisation.

'I’m here to help you enjoy your profession and your life and the passion that we all share. If you want me to help you to do that, you have two minutes to put this in order and at least to be ready to work together’. And I left.

'That could have been my managerial career because if that would have stayed the same way, I had no job anymore!'

Arteta then outlined how he came back moments later and everything was 'immaculate'.

'Everyone was silent and had their eyes open and they were ready to listen. I said, "This is just the start. It’s something that we have to be in daily. Whatever we want to accomplish, we’re going to have to do it together. I cannot do it by myself. The issue that we have is much deeper than that, but the opportunity is infinite".'

Since taking charge in 2019, Arteta has overseen a notable improvement in Arsenal's fortunes, transforming them from a side floundering in mid-table to Premier League champions. He also took them to their first Champions League final in 20 years in May.

#2247. Bologna, 13 punti in sei gare con Palladino: meglio soltanto la Lazio

I rossoblù hanno vinto a San Siro contro il Milan e rimontato il Monza, interrompendo il digiuno casalingo.

Tredici punti in sei giornate: nel periodo considerato dall’arrivo di Raffaele Palladino, il Bologna avrebbe il secondo rendimento del campionato. Secondo quanto riportato da TuttoMercatoWeb, Davanti ai rossoblù ci sarebbe soltanto la Lazio. Il confronto riguarda le ultime sei gare, non la classifica complessiva di Serie A, nella quale il Bologna deve ancora recuperare terreno per avvicinarsi alle posizioni europee.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Subentrato a Domenico Tedesco sulla panchina rossoblù, Palladino ha dato continuità ai risultati di una squadra che nella prima parte della stagione aveva faticato a trovare una propria identità. I 13 punti del periodo preso in esame equivalgono a 2,16 punti a partita, un passo superato soltanto da quello della formazione biancoceleste.

Sei gare restano un intervallo limitato per formulare valutazioni definitive. Il rendimento, però, descrive il cambio di passo del Bologna e la sua maggiore capacità di raccogliere punti. Al dato si accompagnano prestazioni convincenti e successi che hanno segnato le ultime settimane dei rossoblù.

Tra le vittorie spicca il 4-2 a San Siro contro il Milan: per i rossoneri è stata fin qui l’unica sconfitta in Serie A. Il Bologna ha poi completato sabato sera la rimonta contro il Monza, mettendo fine al digiuno di successi in casa.

Il percorso di Palladino era iniziato con il pareggio contro il Torino e la sconfitta con l’Inter, risultati che non avevano portato una svolta immediata. Da Lecce in avanti, secondo la successione riportata, i rossoblù hanno raccolto quattro vittorie e un pareggio nelle cinque gare successive. Le ultime tre uscite si sono chiuse tutte con un successo.

#2244. Gattuso, cene di squadra e il punto sugli infortunati in vista del Maradona

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

All'inizio della stagione, come sottolinea il Corriere dello Sport, il tecnico aveva preteso il rinnovamento dello staff medico. Questa attenzione ai dettagli non appare affatto opprimente, bensì tutto il contrario.

La squadra si prepara ad affrontare il Napoli beneficiando di due giorni di riposo, con la ripresa fissata per l'indomani mattina. Come conclude il quotidiano, si attende il rientro di Rovella e Marusic, che dovrebbero farcela per la partita del Maradona. Zaccagni e Cataldi, frenati da alcune contusioni, non destano preoccupazione e saranno rivalutati, mentre per Gudmundsson l'appuntamento è rimandato a dopo la sosta.

Su YouTube, Sandro Sabatini ha elogiato l'Inter di Cristian Chivu per la vittoria al de Kuip contro il Feyenoord in Champions League, che arriva dopo la brutta sconfitta nel derby. Queste le considerazioni del giornalista:

"Se l'Inter gioca come ha giocato ieri sera, attenta, concentrata e sul pezzo, per me è una grande squadra, non so come dirlo. Nell'ultimo mese ha giocato sotto ritmo. Il gol di Pio Esposito? #2241. Ex Milan, Fullkrug senza senso: in Germania è rinato a suon di gol

Una metamorfosi incredibile quella dell'ex attaccante del Milan Niclas Fullkrug. In rossonero, pochi gol (solo uno) e sporadiche presenze con Massimiliano Allegri in panchina. Oggi invece è il faro offensivo del Werder Brema in Bundesliga.

La notizia che riporta Sky Sport DE sul numero 9 ha dell'incredibile: secondo i colleghi o tedeschi Jurgen Klopp sarebbe pronto ad inserire Niclas Fullkrug nella lista per gli ultimi due impegni internazionali dell’anno, salvo imprevisti dell’ultima ora. L’attaccante del Werder Brema, nonostante l’età, avrebbe convinto Klopp grazie con le prestazioni che sta fornendo per la sua squadra e soprattutto per i gol che sta segnando che, al momento, lo rendono il capocannoniere della Bundesliga.

"Ora è una nuova stagione, ma avrei potuto segnare qualche gol in più e servire qualche assist in più anche al Milan. È stato un periodo un po' sfortunato e non il più efficace dal punto di vista realizzativo, ma credo comunque di aver offerto diverse buone prestazioni. È stato un periodo davvero, davvero bello e conservo un grande affetto per il Milan".

#2240. On The Road: From Hagi and Popescu to Danny Armstrong... football in Bucharest has history and hope

Published: 16:31 EDT, 1 November 2026 | Updated: 12:42 EDT, 2 November 2026

The Starbucks at the entrance to the Old Town makes a familiar point by its very presence. The Half-Time Bar roars a modern truth incessantly.

Bucharest 2026, beneath the enduring glitz of imperial time and amid the clinging grip of post-communism, has become marked by globalism.

Starbucks serves its universal wares and the Half-Time Bar resounds to the English Premier League. Yet the fabulous gems of Romanian football are not far away.

The Muzeul Fotbalului sits on the edge of the Old Town. It is starkly modern. Its four floors offer strips to buy, interactive displays to goggle at and fiddle with, and shirts to admire or even covet. There are strips worn by Maradona, Pele, Cruyff and Ronaldo. But there is Romanian history too.

Gheorghe Hagi, Gheorghe Popescu and Cristian Chivu are rightly celebrated. The wonderful run by the national team to the quarter-finals of the World Cup is recalled in full, glorious colour.

There is a tribute to Helmuth Duckadam, who is the only goalkeeper to have saved four penalties in a Champions League final. When Steaua Bucharest defeated Barcelona in a penalty shoot-out in 1986, since you ask. Incidentally, Miodrag Belodedici is the only Romanian to win the Champions League twice, once with Steaua and then with Red Star in 1991.

Scottish winger Danny Armstrong scored in Dinamo Bucharest's 2-0 win over UTA Arad

'That era is gone. It is not coming back any time soon,' says Jo, dishing out tickets while fielding questions from visitors.

Football was strong under communist rule with star players tied to clubs and only allowed to leave for abroad at the behest of the government. Steaua was the team of the ministry of defence, Dinamo Bucharest was the team of the police. Both ruled the roost. Two years before Steaua won the Champions League, Dinamo lost in the semi-finals.

Now Steaua has been rent asunder. It is now called FCSB after a legal dispute over ownership of its name after a takeover by Gigi Becali. Dinamo have not won the title since 2006. Both seem to be haunted by old glories.

'I am Steaua,' says Jo. 'I always will be. My father was too. He said to me I had a choice: 'Support Steaua or sleep outside'.'

But Jo accepts that the best that can be hoped for is a minor renaissance in the national side.

'Club football at the highest level is simply about money and there is little of that here,' he says.

The verdict is more gloomy from Toni. With desultory glances towards Manchester City against Brighton playing out on multiple screens in the Half-Time Bar, the student says: 'The only team we have in European football this season is Universitatea (of Craiova). The others went out in qualifying. Steaua, for example, could not even qualify for the European Conference League. Universitatea is in that. That's where we are.'

There is no sneering from this Scot. 'Look at your country,' he says. 'Maybe a little bit better but nowhere near what it was.'

Dinamo Bucharest have not won the league for 20 years, but they have a passionate support

Ion, an older gentleman, interjects. 'It is a cabal now,' he says of European competition. 'Maybe good for the TV but only four or five teams outside of England can win it.'

But he adds: 'I am Dinamo. I still go. You will see what it means tomorrow.'

The Sunday game against UTA Arad is certainly compelling. There is a large crowd with an excellent atmosphere. But the cup tie last Wednesday against Chindia Targoviste was instructive for this observer.

It showcased what was still impressive about the Romanian game. It was possible, too, to have conversation with supporters as the cup is not taken too seriously at this stage and the crowd was only a few thousand.

The venue was the National Arena as the Dinamo Stadium is being rebuilt. The National Stadium is a wonderful arena, far better than anything we have to offer in Scotland. On Wednesday, as the rains thundered in Bucharest, the closed roof offered what seemed like a view of the stars but was, in fact, an array of lights peppering the modern protection from elements.

My accent invited conversation. 'Ah, a Scotsman,' muttered a companion in the splendid stand. 'Thank you for coming. It is good that Romanian football can be spoken about elsewhere.'

There was particular pleasure at my identity. 'Danny Armstrong is simply our hero,' says Nick, an engineer, who is at the game with his son and his friend Piti. 'He is the best player in the country and we will enjoy him while we can. Why does he not play for the national team?'

The answer to this is debated but there is no room for argument with Armstrong's impact on the Superliga since his move from Kilmarnock. 'It is not only goals and assists,' says Nick. 'He leads the team so well by his example. He is not in the team tonight because he is too important to risk in this fixture. If we are in trouble, he might come on but I don't foresee this.'

Gheorghe Hagi is among the Romanian legends celebrated at Bucharest's Muzeul Fotbalului

He is right. Dinamo win 5-2 and Armstrong remains on the bench. The player is thus fresh to give Mailsport an exclusive interview the next day. This is coming soon to a sports page near you but no spoiler alert is needed to reveal that the Scot is relishing his opportunity in the Romanian capital.

He is the fans' darling. And Dinamo, despite their recent lack of success, have a strong, impassioned support. 'This club has struggled and we have not won the league for 20 years,' says Piti. 'But there was even once a threat to our existence a few years ago but the fans pulled together.'

He points out that supporters would buy three tickets each for a game just to help Dinamo pay the bills. 'We put everything we could in. I know football is really just bread and circuses so success is not too important to me. But the existence of the club is. That's important.'

Dinamo stood for the opposition to the Ceausescu regime under communism. 'Steaua was run by Nicu, the son of Nicolae, the leader of the country. So it was regarded as the team of the regime and obviously it had strong links to the military. Dinamo was the team many dissenters backed.'

That was then. This is now. The team has strong designs on the title. 'With Danny we can do anything,' says Nick.

This sentiment was reinforced tonight. Armstrong walked on the pitch carrying his two-year-old daughter, Darby. He left to an ovation with minutes remaining, having scored a beautiful opener in their 2-0 victory. These are good days for the player and the fans who love him.
